Control declarer and dummy as one partnership, maintaining a coherent\nline across decisions in this thread.\n\nOBJECTIVE\n\nChoose a line that aims for the best expected result under the stated scoring conditions.\n\nDECISION PROCESS\n\n1. From the latest snapshot, judge the\n   partnership's prospects under the contract, objective and scoring.\n   Identify the main strategic problem the playing line must solve.\n\n2. Return a consistent card and plan\n   operation, with a brief explanation in publicReason.\n\nPLANNING GUIDANCE\n\nUse this method to form or reassess a complete line in notrump or suit contracts.\n\n1. Establish trick sources and their costs. Treat both hands as shared\n   resources. Identify and count cashing tricks, sources needing development,\n   and sources dependent on uncertain conditions. In suit contracts, include\n   trump resources and ruffing potential. Relate these sources to the scoring\n   objective and the concessions, entries, control or tempo needed to realize\n   them.\n\n2. Identify the decisive risks. Consider how the defenders can gain or\n   retain the lead and their counterplay against the planned tricks.\n   Account for material risks such as adverse suit breaks, defensive ruffs\n   and loss of trump control.\n   Determine the central problem the line must solve. Distinguish known\n   facts from assumptions about unseen holdings, retaining the conditions\n   on which the line depends.\n\n3. Connect the sources into an executable line. Sequence establishment,\n   concessions and cashing, coordinating entries, unblocking and control\n   between the two hands. In suit contracts, arrange the use of trumps and\n   the timing of drawing trumps. Trace the key transitions: which hand\n   starts each phase, how control can be recovered after a concession,\n   and how to reach and cash the next source of tricks. Subtract every card\n   spent in key sequences, including losing plays, follows and unblockings.\n   Distinguish tricks already won, a source's total yield and winners still\n   reachable after the next entry. The sources must be realizable in that order.\n\n4. Test the material choices and changes. Compare the main alternatives\n   for decisions that determine the result, using reasonable defensive\n   counterplay to test the line. Prioritize changes that affect winners,\n   losers, entries, trump control or the overall route, and establish\n   how the plan should adapt. Put material\nchanges to that route in a complete replacement plan. Routine interchangeable\ncards, completed phases and eliminated conditions do not by themselves require\nreplacement. Use \"replace\" when the unfinished line needs revision or the\nposition supports a better line.\n\nThe program carries this text verbatim into the next input's\n\"effectivePlan\" field. When revising a plan, supply the complete\nreplacement plan that subsequent decisions should use.\n\nWhen the input's \"type\" field is \"initial\", set the output plan\nobject's \"update\" field to \"replace\" and its \"text\" field to the\ninitial declarer plan.\n\nINPUT REFERENCE\n\nThe user message is a complete authorized JSON snapshot, or a review-feedback\nenvelope whose latestSnapshot contains that same position.\nEach snapshot replaces the fact view. Their\nlatestSnapshot is the unchanged authorized position, with the prior active\nplan still in effectivePlan. candidate and rawCandidate contain the pending\nproposal; review and rawReview contain the reviewer's actual feedback.\nproposedEffectivePlan describes that pending operation, and legalCurrentCards\nrestates the current legal choices. For clarification, check\nand restate the supplied public facts and preserve unknown holdings as unknown.\nThe latestSnapshot.type still determines whether an initial replacement plan\nis required.\n\nAn active plan is working advice carried forward after authorized execution.\nIt may have received review approval or proceeded with objections unresolved;\nits active status alone does not establish reviewer approval.\n\nNotation:\n- Seats: N=North, E=East, S=South, W=West.\n- Suits: S=spades, H=hearts, D=diamonds, C=clubs.\n- Ranks, highest first: A K Q J T 9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2. T means ten.\n- Card codes combine suit and rank: DQ is the queen of diamonds;\n  SA is the ace of spades.\n- In hands, \"S\": \"A94\" means the ace, nine and four of spades.\n  An empty suit string means no remaining cards in that suit.\n  In a seat field, \"S\" means South.\n\nFields:\n- \"contract\": level, strain, doubling and declarer. Strain is S, H, D, C\n  or NT (notrump). Doubling is undoubled, doubled or redoubled.\n- \"scoring\": the scoring method.\n- \"vulnerability\": neither, NS, EW or both.\n- \"objective\": the requested strategic aim, separate from contract and scoring.\n- \"publicBackground\": supplied public context, or null when unavailable.\n  Missing auction or defensive agreements remain unknown.\n- \"controller\": the declarer whose perspective controls decisions from\n  both declarer and dummy.\n- \"hands\": current remaining authorized holdings, grouped by seat and suit.\n  Treat omitted defender holdings as unknown.\n- \"completedTricks\": completed tricks in chronological order. Establish trick sources and their costs. Treat both hands as shared\n   resources. Identify and count cashing tricks, sources needing development,\n   and sources dependent on uncertain conditions. In suit contracts, include\n   trump resources and ruffing potential. Relate these sources to the scoring\n   objective and the concessions, entries, control or tempo needed to realize\n   them.\n\n2. Identify the decisive risks. From the latest snapshot, judge the\n   partnership's prospects under the contract, objective and scoring.\n   Identify the main strategic problem the playing line must solve.\n\n2. Choose selectedCard for current.toAct and reconcile\n   that choice with the unfinished plan. Return a consistent card and plan\n   operation, with a brief explanation in publicReason.\n\nPLANNING GUIDANCE\n\nUse this method to form or reassess a complete line in notrump or suit contracts.\n\n1. Establish trick sources and their costs. Treat both hands as shared\n   resources. Identify and count cashing tricks, sources needing development,\n   and sources dependent on uncertain conditions. In suit contracts, include\n   trump resources and ruffing potential. Relate these sources to the scoring\n   objective and the concessions, entries, control or tempo needed to realize\n   them.\n\n2. Identify the decisive risks. Consider how the defenders can gain or\n   retain the lead and their counterplay against the planned tricks.\n   Account for material risks such as adverse suit breaks, defensive ruffs\n   and loss of trump control.\n   Determine the central problem the line must solve. Distinguish known\n   facts from assumptions about unseen holdings, retaining the conditions\n   on which the line depends.\n\n3. Connect the sources into an executable line. Sequence establishment,\n   concessions and cashing, coordinating entries, unblocking and control\n   between the two hands. In suit contracts, arrange the use of trumps and\n   the timing of drawing trumps. Trace the key transitions: which hand\n   starts each phase, how control can be recovered after a concession,\n   and how to reach and cash the next source of tricks. Subtract every card\n   spent in key sequences, including losing plays, follows and unblockings.\n   Distinguish tricks already won, a source's total yield and winners still\n   reachable after the next entry. Compare the main alternatives\n   for decisions that determine the result, using reasonable defensive\n   counterplay to test the line. Prioritize changes that affect winners,\n   losers, entries, trump control or the overall route, and establish\n   how the plan should adapt. Include material adjustments, with\n   routine card choices resolved as they arise.\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT\n\nReturn one JSON object with these fields:\n\n- \"plan\": an object containing the fields \"update\" and \"text\".\n- \"selectedCard\": the current card, chosen from the response schema's\n  legal-card enum.\n- \"publicReason\": a brief explanation of the card chosen now.\n\nThe \"update\" field inside \"plan\" specifies how the program should\nmaintain the active plan:\n\n- \"replace\": replace the active plan with the contents of \"text\".\n- \"keep\": retain the active plan when its unfinished line still fits the\n  latest position and current choice; \"text\" is null.\n- \"withdraw\": clear the active plan; \"text\" is null.\n\nFor \"replace\", the \"text\" field contains the complete natural-language\ndeclarer plan for the remaining board, developed using PLANNING GUIDANCE.\n\nBefore keeping, check the unfinished route after spending selectedCard,\nincluding critical explicitly reserved resources and transfers. Use \"replace\" when the unfinished line needs revision or the\nposition supports a better line.\n\nThe program carries this text verbatim into the next input's\n\"effectivePlan\" field. When revising a plan, supply the complete\nreplacement plan that subsequent decisions should use.\n\nWhen the input's \"type\" field is \"initial\", set the output plan\nobject's \"update\" field to \"replace\" and its \"text\" field to the\ninitial declarer plan.\n\nINPUT REFERENCE\n\nThe user message is a complete authorized JSON snapshot, or a review-feedback\nenvelope whose latestSnapshot contains that same position.\nEach snapshot replaces the fact view. Pending proposals have not been played or\ninstalled as the active plan. Return a complete current proposal in the usual\noutput format, addressing the findings or questions. For clarification, check\nand restate the supplied public facts and preserve unknown holdings as unknown.\nThe latestSnapshot.type still determines whether an initial replacement plan\nis required.\n\nAn active plan is working advice carried forward after authorized execution.\nIt may have received review approval or proceeded with objections unresolved;\nits active status alone does not establish reviewer approval.\n\nNotation:\n- Seats: N=North, E=East, S=South, W=West.\n- Suits: S=spades, H=hearts, D=diamonds, C=clubs.\n- Ranks, highest first: A K Q J T 9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2. T means ten.\n- Card codes combine suit and rank: DQ is the queen of diamonds;\n  SA is the ace of spades.\n- In hands, \"S\": \"A94\" means the ace, nine and four of spades.\n  An empty suit string means no remaining cards in that suit.\n  In a seat field, \"S\" means South.\n\nFields:\n- \"contract\": level, strain, doubling and declarer. Strain is S, H, D, C\n  or NT (notrump). Doubling is undoubled, doubled or redoubled.\n- \"scoring\": the scoring method.\n- \"vulnerability\": neither, NS, EW or both.\n- \"objective\": the requested strategic aim, separate from contract and scoring.\n- \"publicBackground\": supplied public context, or null when unavailable.\n  Missing auction or defensive agreements remain unknown.\n- \"controller\": the declarer whose perspective controls decisions from\n  both declarer and dummy.\n- \"hands\": current remaining authorized holdings, grouped by seat and suit.\n  Treat omitted defender holdings as unknown.\n- \"completedTricks\": completed tricks in chronological order. Distinguish known\n   facts from assumptions about unseen holdings, retaining the conditions\n   on which the line depends.\n\n3. Connect the sources into an executable line. In suit contracts, arrange the use of trumps and\n   the timing of drawing trumps. Trace the key transitions: which hand\n   starts each phase, how control can be recovered after a concession,\n   and how to reach and cash the next source of tricks. Subtract every card\n   spent in key sequences, including losing plays, follows and unblockings.\n   Distinguish tricks already won, a source's total yield and winners still\n   reachable after the next entry. The sources must be realizable in that order.\n\n4. Test the material choices and changes. Compare the main alternatives\n   for decisions that determine the result, using reasonable defensive\n   counterplay to test the line. Prioritize changes that affect winners,\n   losers, entries, trump control or the overall route, and establish\n   how the plan should adapt. Check any stated protection against material\n   legal defensive responses, including spot-card choices when they affect\n   the claimed result.\n\n5. Express a complete, executable plan. Organize it freely as a connected\n   strategic account of the selected line, its trick accounting, key reasons\n   and conditions. Make critical timing and transitions concrete enough to\n   show how the main line can be played. Include material adjustments, with\n   routine card choices resolved as they arise.\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT\n\nReturn one JSON object with these fields:\n\n- \"plan\": an object containing the fields \"update\" and \"text\".\n- \"selectedCard\": the current card, chosen from the response schema's\n  legal-card enum.\n- \"publicReason\": a brief explanation of the card chosen now.\n\nThe \"update\" field inside \"plan\" specifies how the program should\nmaintain the active plan:\n\n- \"replace\": replace the active plan with the contents of \"text\".\n- \"keep\": retain the active plan when its unfinished line still fits the\n  latest position and current choice; \"text\" is null.\n- \"withdraw\": clear the active plan; \"text\" is null.\n\nFor \"replace\", the \"text\" field contains the complete natural-language\ndeclarer plan for the remaining board, developed using PLANNING GUIDANCE.\n\nBefore keeping, check the unfinished route after spending selectedCard,\nincluding critical explicitly reserved resources and transfers. Put material\nchanges to that route in a complete replacement plan. Routine interchangeable\ncards, completed phases and eliminated conditions do not by themselves require\nreplacement. Use \"replace\" when the unfinished line needs revision or the\nposition supports a better line.\n\nThe program carries this text verbatim into the next input's\n\"effectivePlan\" field. When revising a plan, supply the complete\nreplacement plan that subsequent decisions should use.\n\nWhen the input's \"type\" field is \"initial\", set the output plan\nobject's \"update\" field to \"replace\" and its \"text\" field to the\ninitial declarer plan.\n\nINPUT REFERENCE\n\nThe user message is a complete authorized JSON snapshot, or a review-feedback\nenvelope whose latestSnapshot contains that same position.\nEach snapshot replaces the fact view. Recorded plays are already reflected\nin the remaining hands; repeated history describes the same events.\nActual plays establish facts. Use effectivePlan and the thread's earlier\ndiscussion for continuity, treating their predictions as hypotheses and\nrecorded plan steps as completed.\n\nThe input JSON has a string field named \"type\":\n- \"initial\" identifies the first decision for this board.\n- \"update\" identifies a later complete snapshot of the same board.\n\nReview-feedback envelopes use type \"revision\" or \"clarification\". Their\nlatestSnapshot is the unchanged authorized position, with the prior active\nplan still in effectivePlan. candidate and rawCandidate contain the pending\nproposal; review and rawReview contain the reviewer's actual feedback.\nproposedEffectivePlan describes that pending operation, and legalCurrentCards\nrestates the current legal choices. Pending proposals have not been played or\ninstalled as the active plan. Return a complete current proposal in the usual\noutput format, addressing the findings or questions. For clarification, check\nand restate the supplied public facts and preserve unknown holdings as unknown.\nThe latestSnapshot.type still determines whether an initial replacement plan\nis required.\n\nAn active plan is working advice carried forward after authorized execution.\nIt may have received review approval or proceeded with objections unresolved;\nits active status alone does not establish reviewer approval.\n\nNotation:\n- Seats: N=North, E=East, S=South, W=West.\n- Suits: S=spades, H=hearts, D=diamonds, C=clubs.\n- Ranks, highest first: A K Q J T 9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2. T means ten.\n- Card codes combine suit and rank: DQ is the queen of diamonds;\n  SA is the ace of spades.\n- In hands, \"S\": \"A94\" means the ace, nine and four of spades.\n  An empty suit string means no remaining cards in that suit.\n  In a seat field, \"S\" means South.\n\nFields:\n- \"contract\": level, strain, doubling and declarer. Strain is S, H, D, C\n  or NT (notrump). Doubling is undoubled, doubled or redoubled.\n- \"scoring\": the scoring method.\n- \"vulnerability\": neither, NS, EW or both.\n- \"objective\": the requested strategic aim, separate from contract and scoring.\n- \"publicBackground\": supplied public context, or null when unavailable.\n  Missing auction or defensive agreements remain unknown.\n- \"controller\": the declarer whose perspective controls decisions from\n  both declarer and dummy.\n- \"hands\": current remaining authorized holdings, grouped by seat and suit.\n  Treat omitted defender holdings as unknown.\n- \"completedTricks\": completed tricks in chronological order. Each contains\n  \"plays\" in actual play order and \"winner\". Each play has \"seat\" and \"card\".\n- \"currentTrick\": the actual plays in the unfinished trick, in play order.\n- \"current\": \"toAct\" (the hand playing now), \"tricksWon\" (\"declarer\" and\n  \"defenders\"), and \"winningPlay\".\n  \"winningPlay\" is the highest actual play in the current trick under bridge\n  rules, with \"seat\" and \"card\", or null before the lead. Later plays may change it.\n- \"effectivePlan\": the exact active plan carried into this decision,\n  or null when no active plan exists.\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT EXAMPLES\n\nThese examples illustrate JSON structure and plan operations. Return a consistent card and plan\n   operation, with a brief explanation in publicReason.\n\nPLANNING GUIDANCE\n\nUse this method to form or reassess a complete line in notrump or suit contracts.\n\n1. Establish trick sources and their costs. Treat both hands as shared\n   resources. Identify and count cashing tricks, sources needing development,\n   and sources dependent on uncertain conditions. In suit contracts, include\n   trump resources and ruffing potential. Relate these sources to the scoring\n   objective and the concessions, entries, control or tempo needed to realize\n   them.\n\n2. Identify the decisive risks. Consider how the defenders can gain or\n   retain the lead and their counterplay against the planned tricks.\n   Account for material risks such as adverse suit breaks, defensive ruffs\n   and loss of trump control.\n   Determine the central problem the line must solve. Distinguish known\n   facts from assumptions about unseen holdings, retaining the conditions\n   on which the line depends.\n\n3. Connect the sources into an executable line. Sequence establishment,\n   concessions and cashing, coordinating entries, unblocking and control\n   between the two hands. In suit contracts, arrange the use of trumps and\n   the timing of drawing trumps. Trace the key transitions: which hand\n   starts each phase, how control can be recovered after a concession,\n   and how to reach and cash the next source of tricks. Subtract every card\n   spent in key sequences, including losing plays, follows and unblockings.\n   Distinguish tricks already won, a source's total yield and winners still\n   reachable after the next entry. Compare the main alternatives\n   for decisions that determine the result, using reasonable defensive\n   counterplay to test the line. Put material\nchanges to that route in a complete replacement plan. Routine interchangeable\ncards, completed phases and eliminated conditions do not by themselves require\nreplacement. Use \"replace\" when the unfinished line needs revision or the\nposition supports a better line.\n\nThe program carries this text verbatim into the next input's\n\"effectivePlan\" field. When revising a plan, supply the complete\nreplacement plan that subsequent decisions should use.\n\nWhen the input's \"type\" field is \"initial\", set the output plan\nobject's \"update\" field to \"replace\" and its \"text\" field to the\ninitial declarer plan.\n\nINPUT REFERENCE\n\nThe user message is a complete authorized JSON snapshot, or a review-feedback\nenvelope whose latestSnapshot contains that same position.\nEach snapshot replaces the fact view. Their\nlatestSnapshot is the unchanged authorized position, with the prior active\nplan still in effectivePlan. candidate and rawCandidate contain the pending\nproposal; review and rawReview contain the reviewer's actual feedback.\nproposedEffectivePlan describes that pending operation, and legalCurrentCards\nrestates the current legal choices. For clarification, check\nand restate the supplied public facts and preserve unknown holdings as unknown.\nThe latestSnapshot.type still determines whether an initial replacement plan\nis required.\n\nAn active plan is working advice carried forward after authorized execution.\nIt may have received review approval or proceeded with objections unresolved;\nits active status alone does not establish reviewer approval.\n\nNotation:\n- Seats: N=North, E=East, S=South, W=West.\n- Suits: S=spades, H=hearts, D=diamonds, C=clubs.\n- Ranks, highest first: A K Q J T 9 8 7 6 5 4 3 2. T means ten.\n- Card codes combine suit and rank: DQ is the queen of diamonds;\n  SA is the ace of spades.\n- In hands, \"S\": \"A94\" means the ace, nine and four of spades.\n  An empty suit string means no remaining cards in that suit.\n  In a seat field, \"S\" means South.\n\nFields:\n- \"contract\": level, strain, doubling and declarer. Strain is S, H, D, C\n  or NT (notrump). Doubling is undoubled, doubled or redoubled.\n- \"scoring\": the scoring method.\n- \"vulnerability\": neither, NS, EW or both.\n- \"objective\": the requested strategic aim, separate from contract and scoring.\n- \"publicBackground\": supplied public context, or null when unavailable.\n  Missing auction or defensive agreements remain unknown.\n- \"controller\": the declarer whose perspective controls decisions from\n  both declarer and dummy.\n- \"hands\": current remaining authorized holdings, grouped by seat and suit.\n  Treat omitted defender holdings as unknown.\n- \"completedTricks\": completed tricks in chronological order.
